Well, I'm here because... dispatch and compliance, they've been tracking my route changes through the computer system, you know? And they make these little comments on the radio. It's like they're always watching. Even when people say it's just normal oversight, I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It's funny, because I used to drive the 405 route, and there was this one guy, Dave, who always had something to say about my mud flaps. Like, who cares about mud flaps? Anyway, the other day I saw this stray dog near the depot, just wandering around, looking lost. Reminded me of when I was a kid and we had this golden retriever named Buster. But yeah, the computer system, they're definitely tracking me.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these comments on the radio, do you hear them coming from the actual radio, or are you hearing voices that others around you cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if anyone else hears it. I mean, it sounds like it's coming from the actual radio speaker in the cab, but... I don't know, I haven't asked anyone. Those radios are so outdated anyway. I've been telling dispatch for months that we need new equipment. My truck, it's number 42, has this awful rattle right next to the radio unit every time I hit a bump. \n\nAnd the potholes on the interstate right now are just ridiculous. I hit one last Tuesday that I swear messed up my alignment. I had to take it to the shop, and the mechanic—Frank, he's this older guy who always wears these faded blue overalls—he told me I need to be more careful. Like I can control the city's road maintenance! Frank's alright, though. He gave me a coupon for a free car wash. I haven't even used it yet because it just keeps raining. Have you noticed how much it's been raining this month? My tomato plants on my balcony are completely waterlogged. I was really hoping to make my own salsa this year, but they just look so sad right now...\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic, or do you feel like your thoughts are jumping around rapidly?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if they jump around... I mean, I just talk about what's going on, you know? Like, everything is connected anyway. But people do tell me I talk a lot. My sister, she called me the other day, and she was complaining about her kids. She has three boys, and they are just a handful. The youngest one, Tyler, he actually broke his arm last week falling off a trampoline. I don't even know why people buy those things, they're so dangerous. I remember when I was little, my neighbor had a trampoline, and we'd jump on it while eating popsicles. Cherry was my favorite. But yeah, Tyler's got this bright green cast now, and he had everyone sign it. I haven't gone over to sign it yet because my schedule with dispatch has been so weird with all the route changes... so I guess my thoughts just go where they need to go.\n\n[Last Question]\nAside from these concerns about dispatch, have you had any periods where your mood felt extremely high, or conversely, periods where you felt so low that you couldn't function?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 29 / transportation_worker / associates\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, my mood is just... my mood, you know? Sometimes I'm tired, but that's just because of the shifts. They have me working these weird hours, like starting at 3 AM. Who even wants to be awake at 3 AM? The only place open is that 24-hour diner out by the highway, the one with the neon sign that's always buzzing. I went in there once for coffee, and the waitress—she had this big beehive hair, like from the sixties—she poured my coffee and spilled half of it on the saucer. It just sat there in a little brown puddle. I didn't even want to drink it after that because I hate when the bottom of the mug gets all sticky. It gets on your hands, and then it gets on the steering wheel, and you're just stuck feeling sticky for the next eight hours. But no, I don't get really high or low or anything like that. Just regular, I guess.

I'll have my planner open, but I can't even make myself start dinner or make any phone calls. I just sit there. 

My planner is this really nice leather one, though. I got it at that little stationery shop downtown, the one next to the bakery that always smells like cinnamon rolls. They have the best cinnamon rolls, with that really thick cream cheese frosting. I tried making frosting like that once, but I used too much powdered sugar and it got all gritty. My mom always said you have to sift the sugar first, but who has time for that? She had this old metal sifter with a red handle that squeaked every time you turned it. I always hated that sound, it reminded me of the brakes on my first car. It was this beat-up Honda Civic, and the brakes would squeal every time I stopped at a red light. It was so embarrassing, especially when people would stare. But yeah, I just sit at the table and don't do anything.

It's funny, because I used to drive the 405 route, and there was this one guy, Dave, who always had something to say about my mud flaps. Like, who cares about mud flaps? Anyway, the other day I saw this stray dog near the depot, just wandering around, looking lost. Reminded me of when I was a kid and we had this golden retriever named Buster. He used to chew on everything. Shoes, table legs, you name it. But yeah, the computer system, they're definitely tracking me.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these comments on the radio, do you hear them coming from the actual radio, or are you hearing voices that others around you cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if anyone else hears it. I mean, it sounds like it's coming from the actual radio speaker in the cab, but... I don't know, I haven't asked anyone. Those radios are so outdated anyway. I've been telling dispatch for months that we need new equipment. My truck, it's number 42, has this awful rattle right next to the radio unit every time I hit a bump. \n\nAnd the potholes on the interstate right now are just ridiculous. I hit one last Tuesday that I swear messed up my alignment. I had to take it to the shop, and the mechanic—Frank, he's this older guy who always wears these faded blue overalls—he told me I need to be more careful. Like I can control the city's road maintenance! Frank's alright, though. He gave me a coupon for a free car wash. I haven't even used it yet because it just keeps raining. Have you noticed how much it's been raining this month? My tomato plants on my balcony are completely waterlogged. I was really hoping to make my own salsa this year, but they just look so sad right now...\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic, or do you feel like your thoughts are jumping around rapidly?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if they jump around... I mean, I just talk about what's going on, you know? Like, everything is connected anyway. But people do tell me I talk a lot. My sister, she called me the other day, and she was complaining about her kids. She has three boys, and they are just a handful. The youngest one, Tyler, he actually broke his arm last week falling off a trampoline. I don't even know why people buy those things, they're so dangerous. I remember when I was little, my neighbor had a trampoline, and we'd jump on it while eating popsicles. Cherry was my favorite. But yeah, Tyler's got this bright green cast now, and he had everyone sign it. I haven't gone over to sign it yet because my schedule with dispatch has been so weird with all the route changes... so I guess my thoughts just go where they need to go.\n\nDoctor: Aside from these concerns about dispatch, have you had any periods where your mood felt extremely high, or conversely, periods where you felt so low that you couldn't function?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, my mood is just... my mood, you know? Sometimes I'm tired, but that's just because of the shifts. They have me working these weird hours, like starting at 3 AM. Who even wants to be awake at 3 AM? The only place open is that 24-hour diner out by the highway, the one with the neon sign that's always buzzing. I went in there once for coffee, and the waitress—she had this big beehive hair, like from the sixties—she poured my coffee and spilled half of it on the saucer. It just sat there in a little brown puddle. I didn't even want to drink it after that because I hate when the bottom of the mug gets all sticky. It gets on your hands, and then it gets on the steering wheel, and you're just stuck feeling sticky for the next eight hours. But no, I don't get really high or low or anything like that. Just regular, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have lost interest in things you used to enjoy, or do you find it difficult to motivate yourself to get things done?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah, I just don't really care about doing stuff lately. Like, I used to love singing with my choir, and I'd always have the softball team over to my place, but now I just don't feel like it. Most days after my shift, I just sit at the kitchen table. I'll have my planner open, but I can't even make myself start dinner or make any phone calls. I just sit there. \n\nMy planner is this really nice leather one, though. I got it at that little stationery shop downtown, the one next to the bakery that always smells like cinnamon rolls. They have the best cinnamon rolls, with that really thick cream cheese frosting. I tried making frosting like that once, but I used too much powdered sugar and it got all gritty. My mom always said you have to sift the sugar first, but who has time for that? She had this old metal sifter with a red handle that squeaked every time you turned it. I always hated that sound, it reminded me of the brakes on my first car. It was this beat-up Honda Civic, and the brakes would squeal every time I stopped at a red light. It was so embarrassing, especially when people would stare. But yeah, I just sit at the table and don't do anything.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you feel like your thoughts are sometimes slipping away from you or becoming jumbled in a way that makes it hard for you to keep track of what you are trying to say?\n\nPatient: I don't think they slip away... I just talk, and it all makes sense to me. Sometimes I guess people look at me like they lost track of what I was saying, but I know what I mean. Like yesterday, I was trying to explain to my supervisor about the new route schedule, because they changed the times on the 42 bus line, and that's the one that goes right past the old library. Have you seen what they did to the library? They painted the front doors this really bright, obnoxious yellow. It used to be a nice mahogany wood, really classic looking. My grandfather had a desk made of mahogany. He kept it in his study, and he'd sit there smoking his pipe. It smelled like cherry tobacco. I always liked that smell, it's so much better than cigarettes. The guys at the depot smoke cigarettes all the time on their breaks, and they just toss the butts right on the concrete. It's so messy. I stepped on one the other day and it got stuck in the tread of my boot. I had to use a toothpick to dig it out. But anyway, no, I don't feel like my thoughts get jumbled up. I just have a lot to say, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't really know. I mean, the past six months is a long time to keep track of every single day. I guess they kind of just happen... sometimes together, sometimes not? I don't really keep a calendar of it. I used to have this really nice calendar, though. It was one of those big wall ones with pictures of different national parks for every month. July was Yosemite, and it had this gorgeous picture of a waterfall. I've always wanted to go to Yosemite, but it's so far away, and you have to book those campsites like a year in advance. Who knows what they're going to be doing a year from now? I can barely plan what I'm having for dinner tonight. I was thinking about making spaghetti, but I think the sauce I have in the pantry expired. I hate when things expire before you get to use them. It feels like such a waste of money, you know? Like throwing dollar bills right in the trash. So I'm not really sure about the timeline of everything.

So I'm not really sure about the timeline of everything.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had these experiences of feeling watched or hearing comments on the radio for a long time when you were not feeling down or unmotivated?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 29 / transportation_worker / associates\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, I don't really know. It's hard to separate all that out, like if I was feeling unmotivated or not when the radio stuff was happening. I just know I have the radio on a lot when I'm driving my route. They really do just play the exact same five songs on loop all day on the local station. There's this one country song they always play, and it has this really annoying banjo part. I used to think I wanted to learn how to play the banjo, or maybe the acoustic guitar. My uncle had an acoustic guitar that he kept in his attic, but the strings got all rusted because it was so humid up there. The humidity here has been awful lately, hasn't it? It makes my hair frizz up so bad. I bought this special anti-frizz serum from the drugstore, but it just made my hair feel greasy, like I hadn't washed it in a week. The bottle was really tiny, too, and it cost like fifteen dollars. Everything is getting so expensive now, even just basic stuff at the pharmacy. But yeah, I couldn't tell you if it happened for a long time without the other stuff.

Like I can control the city's road maintenance! Frank's alright, though. He gave me a coupon for a free car wash. I haven't even used it yet because it just keeps raining. Have you noticed how much it's been raining this month? My tomato plants on my balcony are completely waterlogged. I was really hoping to make my own salsa this year, but they just look so sad right now...\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic, or do you feel like your thoughts are jumping around rapidly?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if they jump around... I mean, I just talk about what's going on, you know? Like, everything is connected anyway. But people do tell me I talk a lot. My sister, she called me the other day, and she was complaining about her kids. She has three boys, and they are just a handful. The youngest one, Tyler, he actually broke his arm last week falling off a trampoline. I don't even know why people buy those things, they're so dangerous. I remember when I was little, my neighbor had a trampoline, and we'd jump on it while eating popsicles. Cherry was my favorite. But yeah, Tyler's got this bright green cast now, and he had everyone sign it. I haven't gone over to sign it yet because my schedule with dispatch has been so weird with all the route changes... so I guess my thoughts just go where they need to go.\n\nDoctor: Aside from these concerns about dispatch, have you had any periods where your mood felt extremely high, or conversely, periods where you felt so low that you couldn't function?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, my mood is just... my mood, you know? Sometimes I'm tired, but that's just because of the shifts. They have me working these weird hours, like starting at 3 AM. Who even wants to be awake at 3 AM? The only place open is that 24-hour diner out by the highway, the one with the neon sign that's always buzzing. I went in there once for coffee, and the waitress—she had this big beehive hair, like from the sixties—she poured my coffee and spilled half of it on the saucer. It just sat there in a little brown puddle. I didn't even want to drink it after that because I hate when the bottom of the mug gets all sticky. It gets on your hands, and then it gets on the steering wheel, and you're just stuck feeling sticky for the next eight hours. But no, I don't get really high or low or anything like that. Just regular, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have lost interest in things you used to enjoy, or do you find it difficult to motivate yourself to get things done?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah, I just don't really care about doing stuff lately. Like, I used to love singing with my choir, and I'd always have the softball team over to my place, but now I just don't feel like it. Most days after my shift, I just sit at the kitchen table. I'll have my planner open, but I can't even make myself start dinner or make any phone calls. I just sit there. \n\nMy planner is this really nice leather one, though. I got it at that little stationery shop downtown, the one next to the bakery that always smells like cinnamon rolls. They have the best cinnamon rolls, with that really thick cream cheese frosting. I tried making frosting like that once, but I used too much powdered sugar and it got all gritty. My mom always said you have to sift the sugar first, but who has time for that? She had this old metal sifter with a red handle that squeaked every time you turned it. I always hated that sound, it reminded me of the brakes on my first car. It was this beat-up Honda Civic, and the brakes would squeal every time I stopped at a red light. It was so embarrassing, especially when people would stare. But yeah, I just sit at the table and don't do anything.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you feel like your thoughts are sometimes slipping away from you or becoming jumbled in a way that makes it hard for you to keep track of what you are trying to say?\n\nPatient: I don't think they slip away... I just talk, and it all makes sense to me. Sometimes I guess people look at me like they lost track of what I was saying, but I know what I mean. Like yesterday, I was trying to explain to my supervisor about the new route schedule, because they changed the times on the 42 bus line, and that's the one that goes right past the old library. Have you seen what they did to the library? They painted the front doors this really bright, obnoxious yellow. It used to be a nice mahogany wood, really classic looking. My grandfather had a desk made of mahogany. He kept it in his study, and he'd sit there smoking his pipe. It smelled like cherry tobacco. I always liked that smell, it's so much better than cigarettes. The guys at the depot smoke cigarettes all the time on their breaks, and they just toss the butts right on the concrete. It's so messy. July was Yosemite, and it had this gorgeous picture of a waterfall. I've always wanted to go to Yosemite, but it's so far away, and you have to book those campsites like a year in advance. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, it definitely hasn't been six months. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments, all of that has felt really clear to me for about the past two months. I know it was two months ago because that was right around the time they brought in the new computer monitors at the dispatch depot. They replaced all the old ones with these huge flat screens, and they take up way too much space on the desks. 

My desk at home is really small, so a screen like that would never fit. It's just this little glass one I found at a thrift store a few years ago. I love thrift shopping, you can find the best vintage clothes if you have the patience to dig through the racks. I actually found this amazing denim jacket with all these embroidered patches on the back just last week. One of the patches was a little UFO, which I thought was kind of funny. I used to watch all those sci-fi shows with my older brother when we were little, he was totally obsessed with aliens and space stuff. He actually lives out in New Mexico now, near Albuquerque. The desert is so beautiful out there, the sunsets are just incredible, all these bright pinks and purples. I've always wanted to try painting a sunset like that, but I'm terrible at mixing watercolors. Whenever I try, the colors just bleed together and turn into this ugly, muddy brown mess on the paper. You have to have a really specific type of paper for watercolors, too, otherwise it just warps and gets all wavy. But yeah, it's only been about two months since the computer and radio stuff got really clear.

She had this old metal sifter with a red handle that squeaked every time you turned it. I always hated that sound, it reminded me of the brakes on my first car.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF25.0, F25.1, F20.81\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, I am Dr. Smith. Could you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because... dispatch and compliance, they've been tracking my route changes through the computer system, you know? And they make these little comments on the radio. It's like they're always watching. Even when people say it's just normal oversight, I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It's funny, because I used to drive the 405 route, and there was this one guy, Dave, who always had something to say about my mud flaps. Like, who cares about mud flaps? Anyway, the other day I saw this stray dog near the depot, just wandering around, looking lost. Reminded me of when I was a kid and we had this golden retriever named Buster. He used to chew on everything. Shoes, table legs, you name it. But yeah, the computer system, they're definitely tracking me.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these comments on the radio, do you hear them coming from the actual radio, or are you hearing voices that others around you cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if anyone else hears it. I mean, it sounds like it's coming from the actual radio speaker in the cab, but... I don't know, I haven't asked anyone. Those radios are so outdated anyway. I've been telling dispatch for months that we need new equipment. My truck, it's number 42, has this awful rattle right next to the radio unit every time I hit a bump. \n\nAnd the potholes on the interstate right now are just ridiculous. I hit one last Tuesday that I swear messed up my alignment. I had to take it to the shop, and the mechanic—Frank, he's this older guy who always wears these faded blue overalls—he told me I need to be more careful. Like I can control the city's road maintenance! Frank's alright, though. He gave me a coupon for a free car wash. I haven't even used it yet because it just keeps raining. Have you noticed how much it's been raining this month? My tomato plants on my balcony are completely waterlogged. I was really hoping to make my own salsa this year, but they just look so sad right now...\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic, or do you feel like your thoughts are jumping around rapidly?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if they jump around... I mean, I just talk about what's going on, you know? Like, everything is connected anyway. But people do tell me I talk a lot. My sister, she called me the other day, and she was complaining about her kids. She has three boys, and they are just a handful. The youngest one, Tyler, he actually broke his arm last week falling off a trampoline. I don't even know why people buy those things, they're so dangerous. I remember when I was little, my neighbor had a trampoline, and we'd jump on it while eating popsicles. Cherry was my favorite. But yeah, Tyler's got this bright green cast now, and he had everyone sign it. I haven't gone over to sign it yet because my schedule with dispatch has been so weird with all the route changes... so I guess my thoughts just go where they need to go.\n\nDoctor: Aside from these concerns about dispatch, have you had any periods where your mood felt extremely high, or conversely, periods where you felt so low that you couldn't function?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, my mood is just... my mood, you know? Sometimes I'm tired, but that's just because of the shifts. They have me working these weird hours, like starting at 3 AM. Who even wants to be awake at 3 AM? The only place open is that 24-hour diner out by the highway, the one with the neon sign that's always buzzing. I went in there once for coffee, and the waitress—she had this big beehive hair, like from the sixties—she poured my coffee and spilled half of it on the saucer. It just sat there in a little brown puddle. I didn't even want to drink it after that because I hate when the bottom of the mug gets all sticky. It gets on your hands, and then it gets on the steering wheel, and you're just stuck feeling sticky for the next eight hours. But no, I don't get really high or low or anything like that. Just regular, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have lost interest in things you used to enjoy, or do you find it difficult to motivate yourself to get things done?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah, I just don't really care about doing stuff lately. Like, I used to love singing with my choir, and I'd always have the softball team over to my place, but now I just don't feel like it. Most days after my shift, I just sit at the kitchen table. I'll have my planner open, but I can't even make myself start dinner or make any phone calls. I just sit there. \n\nMy planner is this really nice leather one, though. I got it at that little stationery shop downtown, the one next to the bakery that always smells like cinnamon rolls. They have the best cinnamon rolls, with that really thick cream cheese frosting. I tried making frosting like that once, but I used too much powdered sugar and it got all gritty. My mom always said you have to sift the sugar first, but who has time for that? She had this old metal sifter with a red handle that squeaked every time you turned it. I always hated that sound, it reminded me of the brakes on my first car. It was this beat-up Honda Civic, and the brakes would squeal every time I stopped at a red light. It was so embarrassing, especially when people would stare. But yeah, I just sit at the table and don't do anything.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you feel like your thoughts are sometimes slipping away from you or becoming jumbled in a way that makes it hard for you to keep track of what you are trying to say?\n\nPatient: I don't think they slip away... I just talk, and it all makes sense to me. Sometimes I guess people look at me like they lost track of what I was saying, but I know what I mean. Like yesterday, I was trying to explain to my supervisor about the new route schedule, because they changed the times on the 42 bus line, and that's the one that goes right past the old library. Have you seen what they did to the library? They painted the front doors this really bright, obnoxious yellow. It used to be a nice mahogany wood, really classic looking. My grandfather had a desk made of mahogany. He kept it in his study, and he'd sit there smoking his pipe. It smelled like cherry tobacco. I always liked that smell, it's so much better than cigarettes. The guys at the depot smoke cigarettes all the time on their breaks, and they just toss the butts right on the concrete. It's so messy. I stepped on one the other day and it got stuck in the tread of my boot. I had to use a toothpick to dig it out. But anyway, no, I don't feel like my thoughts get jumbled up. I just have a lot to say, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past six months, have there been any periods where you experienced these concerns about being watched or the radio comments without also feeling the lack of motivation or interest you mentioned?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I mean, the past six months is a long time to keep track of every single day. I guess they kind of just happen... sometimes together, sometimes not? I don't really keep a calendar of it. I used to have this really nice calendar, though. It was one of those big wall ones with pictures of different national parks for every month. July was Yosemite, and it had this gorgeous picture of a waterfall. I've always wanted to go to Yosemite, but it's so far away, and you have to book those campsites like a year in advance. Who knows what they're going to be doing a year from now? I can barely plan what I'm having for dinner tonight. I was thinking about making spaghetti, but I think the sauce I have in the pantry expired. I hate when things expire before you get to use them. It feels like such a waste of money, you know? Like throwing dollar bills right in the trash. So I'm not really sure about the timeline of everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had these experiences of feeling watched or hearing comments on the radio for a long time when you were not feeling down or unmotivated?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really know. It's hard to separate all that out, like if I was feeling unmotivated or not when the radio stuff was happening. I just know I have the radio on a lot when I'm driving my route. They really do just play the exact same five songs on loop all day on the local station. There's this one country song they always play, and it has this really annoying banjo part. I used to think I wanted to learn how to play the banjo, or maybe the acoustic guitar. My uncle had an acoustic guitar that he kept in his attic, but the strings got all rusted because it was so humid up there. The humidity here has been awful lately, hasn't it? It makes my hair frizz up so bad. I bought this special anti-frizz serum from the drugstore, but it just made my hair feel greasy, like I hadn't washed it in a week. The bottle was really tiny, too, and it cost like fifteen dollars. Everything is getting so expensive now, even just basic stuff at the pharmacy. But yeah, I couldn't tell you if it happened for a long time without the other stuff.\n\nDoctor: Can you remember if the feeling that dispatch is tracking you or the comments on the radio started more than six months ago?\n\nPatient: No, it definitely hasn't been six months. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments, all of that has felt really clear to me for about the past two months. I know it was two months ago because that was right around the time they brought in the new computer monitors at the dispatch depot. They replaced all the old ones with these huge flat screens, and they take up way too much space on the desks. \n\nMy desk at home is really small, so a screen like that would never fit. It's just this little glass one I found at a thrift store a few years ago. I love thrift shopping, you can find the best vintage clothes if you have the patience to dig through the racks. I actually found this amazing denim jacket with all these embroidered patches on the back just last week. One of the patches was a little UFO, which I thought was kind of funny. I used to watch all those sci-fi shows with my older brother when we were little, he was totally obsessed with aliens and space stuff. He actually lives out in New Mexico now, near Albuquerque. The desert is so beautiful out there, the sunsets are just incredible, all these bright pinks and purples. I've always wanted to try painting a sunset like that, but I'm terrible at mixing watercolors. Whenever I try, the colors just bleed together and turn into this ugly, muddy brown mess on the paper. You have to have a really specific type of paper for watercolors, too, otherwise it just warps and gets all wavy. But yeah, it's only been about two months since the computer and radio stuff got really clear.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you feel like your mind is jumping from one topic to another so quickly that it might be hard for other people to follow what you are trying to say?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? I mean, I feel like I'm just talking normally. Sometimes I just have a lot to say, I guess. It's like when you're on the phone with someone and the conversation just flows from one thing to the next. I used to talk on the phone with my best friend from high school for hours. Her name was Sarah, and she had this really loud, distinct laugh. You could hear her laughing from all the way down the hallway. She ended up moving to Florida after graduation to go to school for marine biology. She always loved dolphins. We went to SeaWorld once on a middle school trip, and she bought like three different dolphin stuffed animals from the gift shop. I bought a little keychain with a sea turtle on it, but the chain broke off my backpack a few weeks later. Backpacks are made so cheaply now, the zippers always get stuck or the straps fray. I had to buy a new one for work just to carry my lunch and my water bottle. It's this bright neon yellow color, so I guess it's good for visibility at the depot. But no, I don't think my mind is jumping around or anything, I'm just explaining things.\n\nDoctor: During the two months that you have been hearing these radio comments and feeling watched, were you feeling depressed, unmotivated, or low in energy for the majority of that time?\n\nPatient: I don't really think I was depressed or anything. I mean, my energy was fine, I was still getting up and doing my routes every day. Driving takes a lot out of you, though, so you have to drink a ton of coffee to stay alert. I always stop at this little drive-thru place near the depot, they have the best iced lattes. But they started using those paper straws recently, and they always melt in your drink before you can even finish it. It makes the coffee taste like soggy cardboard. \n\nI remember my dad used to build these massive cardboard forts for me and my brother when we were little. We'd take all the leftover appliance boxes from the neighbors and tape them together. We made this huge castle once right after Christmas. Christmas is my favorite holiday, but the decorations are such a pain to put up in my apartment. I have this one string of lights where if just a single bulb goes out, the entire strand goes completely dark. It's so frustrating trying to find the one broken bulb. You just have to sit there testing every single one, and it takes hours. But yeah, I wasn't really feeling low on energy or unmotivated.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

My truck, it's number 42, has this awful rattle right next to the radio unit every time I hit a bump. \n\nAnd the potholes on the interstate right now are just ridiculous. I hit one last Tuesday that I swear messed up my alignment. I had to take it to the shop, and the mechanic—Frank, he's this older guy who always wears these faded blue overalls—he told me I need to be more careful. Like I can control the city's road maintenance! Frank's alright, though. He gave me a coupon for a free car wash. I haven't even used it yet because it just keeps raining. Have you noticed how much it's been raining this month? My tomato plants on my balcony are completely waterlogged. I was really hoping to make my own salsa this year, but they just look so sad right now...\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic, or do you feel like your thoughts are jumping around rapidly?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if they jump around... I mean, I just talk about what's going on, you know? Like, everything is connected anyway. But people do tell me I talk a lot. My sister, she called me the other day, and she was complaining about her kids. She has three boys, and they are just a handful. The youngest one, Tyler, he actually broke his arm last week falling off a trampoline. I don't even know why people buy those things, they're so dangerous. I remember when I was little, my neighbor had a trampoline, and we'd jump on it while eating popsicles. Cherry was my favorite. But yeah, Tyler's got this bright green cast now, and he had everyone sign it. I haven't gone over to sign it yet because my schedule with dispatch has been so weird with all the route changes... so I guess my thoughts just go where they need to go.\n\nDoctor: Aside from these concerns about dispatch, have you had any periods where your mood felt extremely high, or conversely, periods where you felt so low that you couldn't function?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, my mood is just... my mood, you know? Sometimes I'm tired, but that's just because of the shifts. They have me working these weird hours, like starting at 3 AM. Who even wants to be awake at 3 AM? The only place open is that 24-hour diner out by the highway, the one with the neon sign that's always buzzing. I went in there once for coffee, and the waitress—she had this big beehive hair, like from the sixties—she poured my coffee and spilled half of it on the saucer. It just sat there in a little brown puddle. I didn't even want to drink it after that because I hate when the bottom of the mug gets all sticky. It gets on your hands, and then it gets on the steering wheel, and you're just stuck feeling sticky for the next eight hours. But no, I don't get really high or low or anything like that. Just regular, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have lost interest in things you used to enjoy, or do you find it difficult to motivate yourself to get things done?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah, I just don't really care about doing stuff lately. Like, I used to love singing with my choir, and I'd always have the softball team over to my place, but now I just don't feel like it. Most days after my shift, I just sit at the kitchen table. I'll have my planner open, but I can't even make myself start dinner or make any phone calls. I just sit there. \n\nMy planner is this really nice leather one, though. I got it at that little stationery shop downtown, the one next to the bakery that always smells like cinnamon rolls. They have the best cinnamon rolls, with that really thick cream cheese frosting. I tried making frosting like that once, but I used too much powdered sugar and it got all gritty. My mom always said you have to sift the sugar first, but who has time for that? She had this old metal sifter with a red handle that squeaked every time you turned it. I always hated that sound, it reminded me of the brakes on my first car. It was this beat-up Honda Civic, and the brakes would squeal every time I stopped at a red light. It was so embarrassing, especially when people would stare. But yeah, I just sit at the table and don't do anything.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you feel like your thoughts are sometimes slipping away from you or becoming jumbled in a way that makes it hard for you to keep track of what you are trying to say?\n\nPatient: I don't think they slip away... I just talk, and it all makes sense to me. Sometimes I guess people look at me like they lost track of what I was saying, but I know what I mean. Like yesterday, I was trying to explain to my supervisor about the new route schedule, because they changed the times on the 42 bus line, and that's the one that goes right past the old library. Have you seen what they did to the library? They painted the front doors this really bright, obnoxious yellow. It used to be a nice mahogany wood, really classic looking. My grandfather had a desk made of mahogany. He kept it in his study, and he'd sit there smoking his pipe. It smelled like cherry tobacco. I always liked that smell, it's so much better than cigarettes. The guys at the depot smoke cigarettes all the time on their breaks, and they just toss the butts right on the concrete. It's so messy. I stepped on one the other day and it got stuck in the tread of my boot. I had to use a toothpick to dig it out. But anyway, no, I don't feel like my thoughts get jumbled up. I just have a lot to say, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past six months, have there been any periods where you experienced these concerns about being watched or the radio comments without also feeling the lack of motivation or interest you mentioned?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I mean, the past six months is a long time to keep track of every single day. I guess they kind of just happen... sometimes together, sometimes not? I don't really keep a calendar of it. I used to have this really nice calendar, though. It was one of those big wall ones with pictures of different national parks for every month. July was Yosemite, and it had this gorgeous picture of a waterfall. I've always wanted to go to Yosemite, but it's so far away, and you have to book those campsites like a year in advance. Who knows what they're going to be doing a year from now? I can barely plan what I'm having for dinner tonight. I was thinking about making spaghetti, but I think the sauce I have in the pantry expired. I hate when things expire before you get to use them. It feels like such a waste of money, you know? Like throwing dollar bills right in the trash. So I'm not really sure about the timeline of everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had these experiences of feeling watched or hearing comments on the radio for a long time when you were not feeling down or unmotivated?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really know. It's hard to separate all that out, like if I was feeling unmotivated or not when the radio stuff was happening. I just know I have the radio on a lot when I'm driving my route. They really do just play the exact same five songs on loop all day on the local station. There's this one country song they always play, and it has this really annoying banjo part. I used to think I wanted to learn how to play the banjo, or maybe the acoustic guitar. My uncle had an acoustic guitar that he kept in his attic, but the strings got all rusted because it was so humid up there. The humidity here has been awful lately, hasn't it? It makes my hair frizz up so bad. I bought this special anti-frizz serum from the drugstore, but it just made my hair feel greasy, like I hadn't washed it in a week. The bottle was really tiny, too, and it cost like fifteen dollars. Everything is getting so expensive now, even just basic stuff at the pharmacy. But yeah, I couldn't tell you if it happened for a long time without the other stuff.\n\nDoctor: Can you remember if the feeling that dispatch is tracking you or the comments on the radio started more than six months ago?\n\nPatient: No, it definitely hasn't been six months. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments, all of that has felt really clear to me for about the past two months. I know it was two months ago because that was right around the time they brought in the new computer monitors at the dispatch depot. They replaced all the old ones with these huge flat screens, and they take up way too much space on the desks. \n\nMy desk at home is really small, so a screen like that would never fit. It's just this little glass one I found at a thrift store a few years ago. I love thrift shopping, you can find the best vintage clothes if you have the patience to dig through the racks. I actually found this amazing denim jacket with all these embroidered patches on the back just last week. One of the patches was a little UFO, which I thought was kind of funny. I used to watch all those sci-fi shows with my older brother when we were little, he was totally obsessed with aliens and space stuff. He actually lives out in New Mexico now, near Albuquerque. The desert is so beautiful out there, the sunsets are just incredible, all these bright pinks and purples. I've always wanted to try painting a sunset like that, but I'm terrible at mixing watercolors. Whenever I try, the colors just bleed together and turn into this ugly, muddy brown mess on the paper. You have to have a really specific type of paper for watercolors, too, otherwise it just warps and gets all wavy. But yeah, it's only been about two months since the computer and radio stuff got really clear.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you feel like your mind is jumping from one topic to another so quickly that it might be hard for other people to follow what you are trying to say?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? I mean, I feel like I'm just talking normally. Sometimes I just have a lot to say, I guess. It's like when you're on the phone with someone and the conversation just flows from one thing to the next. I used to talk on the phone with my best friend from high school for hours. Her name was Sarah, and she had this really loud, distinct laugh. You could hear her laughing from all the way down the hallway. She ended up moving to Florida after graduation to go to school for marine biology. She always loved dolphins. We went to SeaWorld once on a middle school trip, and she bought like three different dolphin stuffed animals from the gift shop. I bought a little keychain with a sea turtle on it, but the chain broke off my backpack a few weeks later. Backpacks are made so cheaply now, the zippers always get stuck or the straps fray. I had to buy a new one for work just to carry my lunch and my water bottle. It's this bright neon yellow color, so I guess it's good for visibility at the depot. But no, I don't think my mind is jumping around or anything, I'm just explaining things.\n\nDoctor: During the two months that you have been hearing these radio comments and feeling watched, were you feeling depressed, unmotivated, or low in energy for the majority of that time?\n\nPatient: I don't really think I was depressed or anything. I mean, my energy was fine, I was still getting up and doing my routes every day. Driving takes a lot out of you, though, so you have to drink a ton of coffee to stay alert. I always stop at this little drive-thru place near the depot, they have the best iced lattes. But they started using those paper straws recently, and they always melt in your drink before you can even finish it. It makes the coffee taste like soggy cardboard. \n\nI remember my dad used to build these massive cardboard forts for me and my brother when we were little. We'd take all the leftover appliance boxes from the neighbors and tape them together. We made this huge castle once right after Christmas. Christmas is my favorite holiday, but the decorations are such a pain to put up in my apartment. I have this one string of lights where if just a single bulb goes out, the entire strand goes completely dark. It's so frustrating trying to find the one broken bulb. You just have to sit there testing every single one, and it takes hours. But yeah, I wasn't really feeling low on energy or unmotivated.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people told you were not really there?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't seen anything that wasn't there. I mean, my eyesight is pretty good, honestly. I don't even wear glasses, except for sunglasses when I'm driving. The glare off the windshields of other cars can be blinding, especially when the sun is setting right in your eyes. I bought this really nice pair of polarized sunglasses at a gas station once, and they worked perfectly until I accidentally sat on them in the car. They just snapped right in half. \n\nIt's so annoying when things break like that. It reminds me of this old blender I had. I tried to make a smoothie with frozen strawberries, and the motor just completely gave out and started smoking. I had to throw the whole thing away. I love strawberry smoothies, though. There's this little juice bar down the street from my apartment that makes the best ones, they put a little bit of mint in it, which sounds weird but it's actually really refreshing. But no, I haven't seen anything that other people couldn't see.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. No, I don't think so. Before these last couple of months, everything was just... normal, you know? I mean, dispatch has always been kind of annoying, just the way they talk over the radio. Sometimes the static is so bad you can barely make out what they're saying. It reminds me of trying to tune in this old AM radio my grandfather had in his garage. He always said he was going to repaint it, but he could never find the exact right shade of red to match the original factory color. He went to like five different auto body shops looking for it. I actually really like that color red, I used to have a winter coat that was almost the exact same shade. It was super warm, down-filled, but it got a tear in the sleeve on a nail sticking out of a fence, and all the feathers started falling out everywhere. It looked like a bird exploded in my living room. I tried to sew it up, but I'm terrible at sewing. I can't even reattach a button straight. I've been telling dispatch for months that we need new equipment. My truck, it's number 42, has this awful rattle right next to the radio unit every time I hit a bump. \n\nAnd the potholes on the interstate right now are just ridiculous. I hit one last Tuesday that I swear messed up my alignment. I had to take it to the shop, and the mechanic—Frank, he's this older guy who always wears these faded blue overalls—he told me I need to be more careful. Like I can control the city's road maintenance! Frank's alright, though. He gave me a coupon for a free car wash. I haven't even used it yet because it just keeps raining. Have you noticed how much it's been raining this month? My tomato plants on my balcony are completely waterlogged. I was really hoping to make my own salsa this year, but they just look so sad right now...\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic, or do you feel like your thoughts are jumping around rapidly?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if they jump around... I mean, I just talk about what's going on, you know? Like, everything is connected anyway. But people do tell me I talk a lot. My sister, she called me the other day, and she was complaining about her kids. She has three boys, and they are just a handful. The youngest one, Tyler, he actually broke his arm last week falling off a trampoline. I don't even know why people buy those things, they're so dangerous. I remember when I was little, my neighbor had a trampoline, and we'd jump on it while eating popsicles. Cherry was my favorite. But yeah, Tyler's got this bright green cast now, and he had everyone sign it. I haven't gone over to sign it yet because my schedule with dispatch has been so weird with all the route changes... so I guess my thoughts just go where they need to go.\n\nDoctor: Aside from these concerns about dispatch, have you had any periods where your mood felt extremely high, or conversely, periods where you felt so low that you couldn't function?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, my mood is just... my mood, you know? Sometimes I'm tired, but that's just because of the shifts. They have me working these weird hours, like starting at 3 AM. Who even wants to be awake at 3 AM? The only place open is that 24-hour diner out by the highway, the one with the neon sign that's always buzzing. I went in there once for coffee, and the waitress—she had this big beehive hair, like from the sixties—she poured my coffee and spilled half of it on the saucer. It just sat there in a little brown puddle. I didn't even want to drink it after that because I hate when the bottom of the mug gets all sticky. It gets on your hands, and then it gets on the steering wheel, and you're just stuck feeling sticky for the next eight hours. But no, I don't get really high or low or anything like that. Just regular, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have lost interest in things you used to enjoy, or do you find it difficult to motivate yourself to get things done?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah, I just don't really care about doing stuff lately. Like, I used to love singing with my choir, and I'd always have the softball team over to my place, but now I just don't feel like it. Most days after my shift, I just sit at the kitchen table. I'll have my planner open, but I can't even make myself start dinner or make any phone calls. I just sit there. \n\nMy planner is this really nice leather one, though. I got it at that little stationery shop downtown, the one next to the bakery that always smells like cinnamon rolls. They have the best cinnamon rolls, with that really thick cream cheese frosting. I tried making frosting like that once, but I used too much powdered sugar and it got all gritty. My mom always said you have to sift the sugar first, but who has time for that? She had this old metal sifter with a red handle that squeaked every time you turned it. I always hated that sound, it reminded me of the brakes on my first car. It was this beat-up Honda Civic, and the brakes would squeal every time I stopped at a red light. It was so embarrassing, especially when people would stare. But yeah, I just sit at the table and don't do anything.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you feel like your thoughts are sometimes slipping away from you or becoming jumbled in a way that makes it hard for you to keep track of what you are trying to say?\n\nPatient: I don't think they slip away... I just talk, and it all makes sense to me. Sometimes I guess people look at me like they lost track of what I was saying, but I know what I mean. Like yesterday, I was trying to explain to my supervisor about the new route schedule, because they changed the times on the 42 bus line, and that's the one that goes right past the old library. Have you seen what they did to the library? They painted the front doors this really bright, obnoxious yellow. It used to be a nice mahogany wood, really classic looking. My grandfather had a desk made of mahogany. He kept it in his study, and he'd sit there smoking his pipe. It smelled like cherry tobacco. I always liked that smell, it's so much better than cigarettes. The guys at the depot smoke cigarettes all the time on their breaks, and they just toss the butts right on the concrete. It's so messy. I stepped on one the other day and it got stuck in the tread of my boot. I had to use a toothpick to dig it out. But anyway, no, I don't feel like my thoughts get jumbled up. I just have a lot to say, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past six months, have there been any periods where you experienced these concerns about being watched or the radio comments without also feeling the lack of motivation or interest you mentioned?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I mean, the past six months is a long time to keep track of every single day. I guess they kind of just happen... sometimes together, sometimes not? I don't really keep a calendar of it. I used to have this really nice calendar, though. It was one of those big wall ones with pictures of different national parks for every month. July was Yosemite, and it had this gorgeous picture of a waterfall. I've always wanted to go to Yosemite, but it's so far away, and you have to book those campsites like a year in advance. Who knows what they're going to be doing a year from now? I can barely plan what I'm having for dinner tonight. I was thinking about making spaghetti, but I think the sauce I have in the pantry expired. I hate when things expire before you get to use them. It feels like such a waste of money, you know? Like throwing dollar bills right in the trash. So I'm not really sure about the timeline of everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had these experiences of feeling watched or hearing comments on the radio for a long time when you were not feeling down or unmotivated?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really know. It's hard to separate all that out, like if I was feeling unmotivated or not when the radio stuff was happening. I just know I have the radio on a lot when I'm driving my route. They really do just play the exact same five songs on loop all day on the local station. There's this one country song they always play, and it has this really annoying banjo part. I used to think I wanted to learn how to play the banjo, or maybe the acoustic guitar. My uncle had an acoustic guitar that he kept in his attic, but the strings got all rusted because it was so humid up there. The humidity here has been awful lately, hasn't it? It makes my hair frizz up so bad. I bought this special anti-frizz serum from the drugstore, but it just made my hair feel greasy, like I hadn't washed it in a week. The bottle was really tiny, too, and it cost like fifteen dollars. Everything is getting so expensive now, even just basic stuff at the pharmacy. But yeah, I couldn't tell you if it happened for a long time without the other stuff.\n\nDoctor: Can you remember if the feeling that dispatch is tracking you or the comments on the radio started more than six months ago?\n\nPatient: No, it definitely hasn't been six months. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments, all of that has felt really clear to me for about the past two months. I know it was two months ago because that was right around the time they brought in the new computer monitors at the dispatch depot. They replaced all the old ones with these huge flat screens, and they take up way too much space on the desks. \n\nMy desk at home is really small, so a screen like that would never fit. It's just this little glass one I found at a thrift store a few years ago. I love thrift shopping, you can find the best vintage clothes if you have the patience to dig through the racks. I actually found this amazing denim jacket with all these embroidered patches on the back just last week. One of the patches was a little UFO, which I thought was kind of funny. I used to watch all those sci-fi shows with my older brother when we were little, he was totally obsessed with aliens and space stuff. He actually lives out in New Mexico now, near Albuquerque. The desert is so beautiful out there, the sunsets are just incredible, all these bright pinks and purples. I've always wanted to try painting a sunset like that, but I'm terrible at mixing watercolors. Whenever I try, the colors just bleed together and turn into this ugly, muddy brown mess on the paper. You have to have a really specific type of paper for watercolors, too, otherwise it just warps and gets all wavy. But yeah, it's only been about two months since the computer and radio stuff got really clear.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you feel like your mind is jumping from one topic to another so quickly that it might be hard for other people to follow what you are trying to say?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? I mean, I feel like I'm just talking normally. Sometimes I just have a lot to say, I guess. It's like when you're on the phone with someone and the conversation just flows from one thing to the next. I used to talk on the phone with my best friend from high school for hours. Her name was Sarah, and she had this really loud, distinct laugh. You could hear her laughing from all the way down the hallway. She ended up moving to Florida after graduation to go to school for marine biology. She always loved dolphins. We went to SeaWorld once on a middle school trip, and she bought like three different dolphin stuffed animals from the gift shop. I bought a little keychain with a sea turtle on it, but the chain broke off my backpack a few weeks later. Backpacks are made so cheaply now, the zippers always get stuck or the straps fray. I had to buy a new one for work just to carry my lunch and my water bottle. It's this bright neon yellow color, so I guess it's good for visibility at the depot. But no, I don't think my mind is jumping around or anything, I'm just explaining things.\n\nDoctor: During the two months that you have been hearing these radio comments and feeling watched, were you feeling depressed, unmotivated, or low in energy for the majority of that time?\n\nPatient: I don't really think I was depressed or anything. I mean, my energy was fine, I was still getting up and doing my routes every day. Driving takes a lot out of you, though, so you have to drink a ton of coffee to stay alert. I always stop at this little drive-thru place near the depot, they have the best iced lattes. But they started using those paper straws recently, and they always melt in your drink before you can even finish it. It makes the coffee taste like soggy cardboard. \n\nI remember my dad used to build these massive cardboard forts for me and my brother when we were little. We'd take all the leftover appliance boxes from the neighbors and tape them together. We made this huge castle once right after Christmas. Christmas is my favorite holiday, but the decorations are such a pain to put up in my apartment. I have this one string of lights where if just a single bulb goes out, the entire strand goes completely dark. It's so frustrating trying to find the one broken bulb. You just have to sit there testing every single one, and it takes hours. But yeah, I wasn't really feeling low on energy or unmotivated.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people told you were not really there?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't seen anything that wasn't there. I mean, my eyesight is pretty good, honestly. I don't even wear glasses, except for sunglasses when I'm driving. The glare off the windshields of other cars can be blinding, especially when the sun is setting right in your eyes. I bought this really nice pair of polarized sunglasses at a gas station once, and they worked perfectly until I accidentally sat on them in the car. They just snapped right in half. \n\nIt's so annoying when things break like that. It reminds me of this old blender I had. I tried to make a smoothie with frozen strawberries, and the motor just completely gave out and started smoking. I had to throw the whole thing away. I love strawberry smoothies, though. There's this little juice bar down the street from my apartment that makes the best ones, they put a little bit of mint in it, which sounds weird but it's actually really refreshing. But no, I haven't seen anything that other people couldn't see.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the concerns with the radio and dispatch have been clear for about two months; before those two months, did you ever experience these same feelings of being watched or hearing comments on the radio at any point in the past?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Before these last couple of months, everything was just... normal, you know? I mean, dispatch has always been kind of annoying, just the way they talk over the radio. Sometimes the static is so bad you can barely make out what they're saying. It reminds me of trying to tune in this old AM radio my grandfather had in his garage. He used to spend hours out there fixing up this vintage Chevy truck. It was this beautiful cherry red color, but the paint was chipping on the hood. \n\nHe always said he was going to repaint it, but he could never find the exact right shade of red to match the original factory color. He went to like five different auto body shops looking for it. I actually really like that color red, I used to have a winter coat that was almost the exact same shade. It was super warm, down-filled, but it got a tear in the sleeve on a nail sticking out of a fence, and all the feathers started falling out everywhere. It looked like a bird exploded in my living room. I tried to sew it up, but I'm terrible at sewing. I can't even reattach a button straight. But yeah, before two months ago, I never noticed the radio comments or felt like anyone was watching me. Everything was just regular work stuff.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
